    try:
        base = kwargs.get('height_sequence_1', globals().get('height_sequence_1'))
        if base is not None:
            n = len(base)
            if n > 0:

    return [1.0] * 4096

Key algorithm details:

Could implement a simple inner loop:
```
it=0
beta = 20.0
no_improve=0
while time<deadline:
    c = fast_conv(p)
    cmax = c.max()
    score = 2n*cmax
    if score < best_score: best=...
    if cmax <= 0: break
    # occasionally direct validate
    tau = cmax / beta
    w = stable_softmax(c, tau)
    g = corr(p,w)
    gmin = g.min()
    grange = g.max()-gmin
    if grange < 1e-15:
        # perturb
        p = add_noise(p, sigma)
        continue
    for exp_range in [0.05,0.2,0.5,1.0]:
        eta = exp_range / grange
        p_new = p * exp(-eta*(g-gmin))
        p_new = floor_normalize(p_new, floor)
        c_new = fast_conv(p_new)
        sc = 2*n*c_new.max()
        if sc < current_score: choose
    if best_candidate chosen:
        p = p_new
        no_improve=0
    else:
        no_improve+=1
        # maybe adjust beta, add noise
        if no_improve % 10 ==0: beta = max(beta_min, beta*0.8) or random
        p = mix(p, noise, 0.05)
```

B. Block random local search on full p.

Use multiplicative updates with random active weights. To escape analytic local min, we can do "temperature annealing" where weights w are random over coefficients, not just top c. E.g., choose a random lag k (or random subset) and apply multiplicative gradient for that constraint, line search. This is like coordinate descent on constraints. Some random single-lag updates may move away and lower global max.

Algorithm random constraint descent:
- Given p, c, score.
- Choose a set of lags L: maybe top K plus random K.
- For each lag k, gradient g_j = p_{k-j} (valid). Update p_j *= exp(-eta*g_j/min?); normalize; line search. This attempts to reduce c_k.
- If c is above current target, it may improve. But reducing one lag can increase others. With multiple random tries keep best.

This is cheap (one corr per random lag? Actually can compute g directly O(n) for a lag: g_j = p_{k-j} for valid j). We can try many single-lag updates. Starting from base, reducing a single prefix lag shifts mass opposite; may find escape.

More generally, choose random weights w_k (e.g., Dirichlet over all lags) and do multiplicative update. This explores. Since gradient cost O(n) if w sparse/random dense? For dense w, use FFT O(n log n). We can generate random sparse w over K random lags and update. This may find directions not stationary.

C. Full multiplicative optimization with adaptive random weights.

Loop:
- Compute c.
- Build weights w in several ways:
  1. top soft (logsum) as before.
  2. random subset of lags chosen with probability proportional to c^q (or uniform).
  3. single max lag.
  4. reverse of p? 
- For each, compute g (FFT or O(K n) if sparse), exponent_range line search, evaluate; keep best.
- If no improvement, random perturb.

This is more expensive but might find better.

D. Direct use of `evaluate_sequence` for best candidates to avoid FFT bias. We can call for top few at end.

```
gmin = g.min()
grange = g.max()-gmin
if grange <=0: return None
p_new0 = p
best_local = p, score
for exp_range in [0.02,0.1,0.3,1.0,3.0]:
    eta = exp_range / grange
    logfac = -eta*(g-gmin)
    # clip logfac to [-50,50]
    fac = np.exp(np.clip(logfac, -50, 50))
    p_new = p*fac
    floor = max(1e-15, p.max()*1e-10)
    p_new = np.maximum(p_new, floor)
    p_new /= p_new.sum()
    sc = fast_score(p_new)
    if sc < best_local_score: choose
return best
```

Soft weights function:
```
def make_soft_weights(c, beta):
    cmax = c.max()
    if cmax <=0: return np.full(len(c),1/len(c))
    tau = cmax / beta
    x = (c - cmax)/tau
    x = x - x.max()? already max 0
    # clip x < -50
    x = np.clip(x, -50, 0)
    w = np.exp(x)
    s = w.sum()
    if s <=0: w[np.argmax(c)] = 1
    else: w /= s
    return w
```

Let's write helpers:
```
def now(): return time.monotonic()
def next_pow2(x): n=1; while n<x: n<<=1; return n
def fast_convolve(p):
    n=p.size; size=next_pow2(2*n-1)
    f=np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(f*f, size)[:2*n-1]
    if c.size:
        # FFT roundoff: set tiny negative to 0 if >-1e-12*max
        cmax = c.max()
        if cmax > 0:
            c = np.where(c < 0, 0.0, c)
    return c
def fast_score(p):
    c=fast_convolve(p)
    cmax=c.max()
    if cmax <=0: return np.inf
    return 2.0*len(p)*float(cmax)
```
`np.fft.rfft` uses complex double. Good.

`normalize_p(p, floor=None)`:
```
p = np.asarray(p,float)
p = np.where(np.isfinite(p)&(p>0), p, 0)
s=p.sum()
if s<=0: return np.full_like(p,1/n)
p=p/s
if floor is None: floor=max(1e-16, p.max()*1e-10)
p=np.maximum(p, floor)
p /= p.sum()
return p
```
But if we always floor, base min ~1/√n/n? For n4096 min ~1/(2n√n) ~7e-6? floor 1e-16 no effect. Good.

```
a = np.empty(n)

return normalize_p(a)
```

`soft_local_search(p, n, rng, time_limit)`:
We can implement a loop as above. It will be called for each n.
Pseudo:
```
def soft_local_search(p, n, rng, deadline):
    p = normalize_p(p)
    best = p.copy(); best_score = fast_score(p)
    no_improve = 0
    beta = 20.0
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = fast_convolve(p)
        cmax = c.max(); score=2*n*cmax
        if score < best_score - 1e-15:
            best=p.copy(); best_score=score; no_improve=0
        it +=1
        # choose beta with schedule/random
        # Try several weights:
        improved = False
        # 1 soft top
        for beta_try in [beta, beta*0.5, beta*2, 50.0, 2.0]:
            w = soft_weights(c, beta_try)
            g = corr_fft(p,w)
            cand, sc = gradient_update(p,g, [0.05,0.2,0.8])
            if sc < best_score:
                p=cand; best_score=sc; best=p.copy(); improved=True; beta=beta_try; beta=max(1,beta*0.8); break
        if improved: continue
        # 2 random sparse weights
        c_norm = c / cmax
        choose K e.g. 10 lags; weights proportional c_norm^8 or random
        g = sparse_gradient(p, lag_weights)
        cand, sc = gradient_update(p,g, [0.1,0.5,2])
        if sc < best_score: ...
        # 3 random perturbation
        if no_improve > 0: no_improve +=1
        else: no_improve=0
        if no_improve > 20 or rng.random()<0.05:
            sigma = 0.05 * (1 + min(no_improve,20)*0.1)
            noise = rng.normal(0, sigma, size=n)
            cand = p*np.exp(noise)
            cand=normalize_p(cand)
            sc=fast_score(cand)
            if sc < best_score: p=cand; best_score=sc; best=p.copy(); no_improve=0
            else: no_improve +=1
            if no_improve > 100: beta = rng.uniform(1,100); no_improve=0
        if not improved and no_improve <=20:
            # small noise anyway
```

`gradient_update(p,g, ranges)`:
```
def gradient_update(p,g,ranges):
    best_p=p; best_sc=fast_score(p)
    g=g - g.min()
    grange=g.max()
    if grange <= 1e-18: return p,best_sc
    # If grange too small? scale
    for er in ranges:
       eta=er/grange
       fac=np.exp(np.clip(-eta*g, -50, 50))
       pn=normalize_p(p*fac)
       sc=fast_score(pn)
       if sc<best_sc: best_p=pn; best_sc=sc
    return best_p,best_sc
```

`corr_fft(p,w)`:
Need nfft for 3n-2. Use complex FFT? `np.fft.rfft` on real w_rev and p, multiply, irfft.
```
def corr_fft(p,w):
    n=p.size; L=2*n-1
    size=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], size)
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fw*fp, size)[:3*n-2]
    g=conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    return 2.0*g  # factor irrelevant
```
Check memory: size 32768 for n8192, arrays 32768 complex ~0.5MB each, fine.

```
def sparse_gradient(p, lags_weights):
    n=p.size; g=np.zeros(n)
    for k,wval in lags_weights:
       if wval==0: continue
       lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); hi=min(n-1,k)
       # if hi<lo continue
       # idx = k - np.arange(lo,hi+1)
       # Use reverse slice:
       start=k-lo
       stop=k-hi
       # Need slice p[start:stop-1:-1] gives start, start-1,... stop
       # if stop<start.
       g[lo:hi+1] += 2.0*wval*p[start:stop-1:-1] if hi>lo else (2*wval*p[k])
    return g
```

```
def normalize_p(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,dtype=float)
   p=np.where(np.isfinite(p) & (p>0), p, 0.0)
   s=p.sum()
   if s<=0: return np.full(p.shape,1.0/p.size)
   return p/s
```

Final scale:
```
def scale_for_evaluate(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,float)
   mx=p.max()
   if mx<=0: return [1.0]*100? 
   # want sum maybe n, but avoid >1000.
   scale = min(1000.0/mx, float(len(p)))
   # also ensure sum>=0.01: if scale*sum <0.01, scale up
   s=p.sum()
   if scale*s < 0.01: scale = 0.01/s
   # cap 1000/mx
   scale = min(scale, 1000/mx)
   if scale <=0: scale=1
   return [float(x*scale) for x in p]
```
Since p sum=1, scale=n gives values ~1, sum=n. If mx large >1000/n, scale=1000/mx, sum may be less than 0.01? For n large mx large? If p has one mass >1000/n, e.g., mx=0.5,n4096, scale=2000? cap 2000? 1000/0.5=2000, sum=2000>0.01. Good. If n small mx=0.9 scale1111, okay. Use python list.

```
def fourier_hill_climb(n, base, rng, deadline):
   base_log = np.log(base+1e-300)
   basis = make_basis(n)
   p0 = base; score0=fast_score(base)
   best_params=np.zeros(1+2*len(freqs)); best_score=score0; best_p=base.copy()
   it=0
   step=0.5
   no_improve=0
   while time<deadline:
       # random coordinate or few
       j = rng.integers(0, len(best_params))
       # try values around current: deltas = step * [0.1,0.3,1,3,10] * ±
       best_local=best_params.copy(); best_local_score=best_score
       for sign in [-1,1]:
          for mult in [0.1,0.3,1,3,10]:
             trial=best_params.copy(); trial[j]+=sign*step*mult
             p=make_fourier(trial,...); sc=fast_score(p)
             if sc<best_local_score: ...
       if best_local_score < best_score - 1e-12:
          accept; no_improve=0
       else:
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve>50: step*=0.5; no_improve=0
       # occasionally random jump
       if rng.random()<0.05:
          trial = best_params + rng.normal(0, 0.5*max(step,0.1), size=best_params.size)
          p=...; sc=...
          if sc<best_score: accept
       # occasional reset to base? 
   return best_p,best_score,best_params
```

Let's consider numerical stability of `normalize_p` after exp: If p contains nan/inf, handle.
```
def normalize_p(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size ==0: return p
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p) & (p > 0.0), p, 0.0)
    s = p.sum(dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0: return np.full(p.shape, 1.0/p.size)
    return p / s
```

`local_search` detailed:
```
def local_search(n, p, rng, deadline, max_iter=None):
    p=normalize_p(p.copy())
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)
    c=fast_convolve(p); score=...
    no_improve=0
    beta=20.0
    iter=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iter+=1
        c=fast_convolve(p)
        cmax=c.max(); score=2*n*cmax
        if score < best_score - 1e-13:
            best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score; no_improve=0
        # If best_score near target? still continue maybe.
        # Try soft weights with a few betas
        improved=False
        # choose betas based on beta and random
        beta_tries = unique([max(1.0,beta), max(1.0,beta/3), max(1.0,beta*3), 2.0, 50.0])
        for b in beta_tries[:4]:
            w=soft_weights(c,b)
            g=corr_fft(p,w)
            cand, sc = gradient_update(p,g,[0.1,0.3,1.0],score)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
               best_p=cand; best_score=sc; p=cand; no_improve=0; improved=True
               # adapt beta
               beta = b
               break
        if improved: continue
        # Sparse random weights
        # choose K random, at least one max
        K = int(rng.integers(3, 17))
        idx = rng.choice(2*n-1, size=K, replace=False)
        # ensure max included
        idx[0] = int(np.argmax(c))
        wvals = c[idx]
        wvals = np.maximum(wvals, 1e-300)
        wvals = wvals ** 6
        s=wvals.sum()
        if s>0: wvals/=s
        g = sparse_gradient(p, list(zip(idx,wvals))) # can't use zip? zip okay not lambda. But tuple list.
        cand, sc = gradient_update(p,g,[0.05,0.2,0.6],score)
        if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
           ... continue
        # No improvement: perturb
        no_improve +=1
        # Choose perturbation size increasing with no_improve
        sigma = 0.05 + 0.02*min(no_improve,50)
        noise = rng.normal(0, sigma, size=n)
        cand = normalize_p(p * np.exp(noise))
        sc = fast_score(cand)
        if sc < best_score - 1e-13:
           p=cand; best_p=cand; best_score=sc; no_improve=0
        else:
           # try random smooth Fourier perturbation? maybe expensive.
           if no_improve % 20 == 0:
               # random direction with low freq
               phase = rng.normal(0,1,size=8)
               k = np.arange(n)
               pert = np.zeros(n)
               for m,a in enumerate(phase,1): pert += a*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*k/(n-1)+rng.uniform(0,6.28))
               cand=normalize_p(p*np.exp(0.3*pert))
               sc=fast_score(cand)
               if sc < best_score: p=cand; best=...; no_improve=0
           # update beta random
           if no_improve % 10 ==0: beta = float(rng.uniform(1,100))
           if no_improve > 200:
               # reset to best_p? or return? To avoid destroying, p=best_p.copy(); no_improve=0? but may loop.
               p=best_p.copy()
               no_improve=0
        if no_improve > 5000: break
    return best_p,best_score
```

Implement `random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amplitude)`: returns vector = ∑ rng.normal(0, amplitude)*basis[m] for selected m. But top-level.
```
def random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amplitude, n_modes=None):
   m = len(basis)
   k = min(m, int(rng.integers(1, n_modes+1)))
   idx = rng.choice(m, size=k, replace=False)
   vec = np.zeros(n)
   for i in idx:
       vec += rng.normal(0, amplitude)*basis[i]
   return vec
```

Scale sequence: If p has very small values, after scale maybe underflow? No.
```
def scale_sequence(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,float); n=len(p); mx=p.max(); s=p.sum()
   if mx <=0 or s<=0: return [1.0]*max(1,n)
   scale = float(n)
   # ensure mx*scale <=1000
   if mx > 1000.0/scale: scale = 1000.0/mx
   # ensure sum*scale >=0.01
   if s*scale <0.01: scale = 0.01/s
   if mx > 1000.0/scale: scale = 1000.0/mx
   if scale <=0 or not finite: scale=1.0
   return [float(x*scale) for x in p]
```
If p has max 0.5, n4096 scale=1000/0.5=2000, sum=2000, okay. If n small 1, scale min(1000/mx,1). p sum1, mx1, scale1, sum1>=0.01.

Pseudo local_search:
```
def local_search(n,p,rng,deadline,basis=None):
    p=normalize_p(p.copy())
    c=fast_convolve_arr(p)
    T=1/(2*n-1)
    var=np.sum((c-T)**2)
    max_score=2*n*c.max()
    best_p=p.copy(); best_max=max_score; best_var=var
    cur_var=var; no_improve=0; beta=20
    while time<deadline:
       # current c, var, max_score should be for p
       improved=False
       # generate candidate list: (g, ranges, maybe weight_type)
       # 1 variance linear
       dev = c - T
       g = corr_fft(p, dev)
       for er in [0.02,0.05,0.15,0.4]:
           cand = make_update(p,g,er)
           c2=fast_convolve_arr(cand); max2=2*n*c2.max(); var2=np.sum((c2-T)**2)
           if max2 < best_max - 1e-12:
              accept best; improved=True; break
           elif max2 <= best_max + 0.03 and var2 < cur_var - 1e-12:
              p=cand; cur_var=var2; improved=True; break
       if improved: continue
       # 2 variance signed squared
       dev2 = dev * np.abs(dev)
       g=corr_fft(p, dev2)
       for er in [0.01,0.03,0.1]: ...
       # 3 soft top
       for b in ...
       # 4 sparse
       # no improve perturb
```
This is many `fast_convolve` calls (4*4=16 for variance plus others). Could be heavy. Use fewer ranges: variance [0.05,0.2,0.6], signed [0.02,0.1,0.3], soft [0.1,0.3,1.0], sparse [0.05,0.2]. Total 12 conv per iteration. If iteration takes 0.05s =0.6s, 1000 iter=600s. Okay maybe but high. We can reduce: pick one weight type based on phase: Initially focus variance until max improves; then focus soft/sparse.

Need `make_update` helper:
```
def multiplicative_update(p, g, exp_range):
    g = np.asarray(g,float)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)): return p
    gmin = np.min(g)
    grange = np.max(g)-gmin
    if grange <= 1e-18: return p
    gs = (g-gmin)/grange
    fac = np.exp(-exp_range*gs)
    return normalize_p(p*fac)
```
This is top-level. No lambda.

In local_search, use it.

```
def local_search(n, p, rng, deadline, basis=None):
   p=normalize_p(p.copy())
   T_size=next_pow2(2*n-1); C_size=next_pow2(3*n-2)
   c=fast_convolve_arr(p,T_size)
   mean=c.mean(); var=np.sum((c-mean)**2); best_max=2*n*c.max(); best_p=p.copy(); best_var=var; cur_var=var; no=0
   iter=0
   while time<deadline:
      iter+=1
      spec_p=np.fft.rfft(p,C_size)
      improved=False
      if iter < 600:
          strat = (iter%2) # 0 var1,1 var2
          if strat==0:
             w=c-c.mean(); ranges=[0.03,0.1,0.3,0.8]
          else:
             dev=c-c.mean(); w=dev*np.abs(dev); ranges=[0.01,0.03,0.1,0.3]
          # try ranges, accept if max better or var better with max margin
          for er in ranges:
             cand=multiplicative_update(p,w,er)
             ... consider
             if improved: break
      else:
          rot = iter % 4
          if rot==0: w=c-c.mean(); ranges=[0.05,0.2,0.6]
          elif rot==1: dev=c-c.mean(); w=dev*np.abs(dev); ranges=[0.02,0.08,0.25]
          elif rot==2: w=soft_weights(c, 20.0); ranges=[0.1,0.3,1.0]
          else: sparse
      if improved:
          # recompute c? candidate c2 available from last cand; set c=c2, var=var2, mean=c.mean()? We need update c for next iter. We can set c = c2, var=var2 after improved.
          continue
      # no improve: perturb
      no +=1
      ...
   # after loop maybe compute final score for best_p
   return best_p, best_max
```

`random_restart` simply:
```
def random_restart_p(base, basis, rng, amplitude_min, amplitude_max):
    plog = np.log(base+1e-300)
    # random low-dim perturbation
    k=int(rng.integers(2, len(basis)+1))
    idx=rng.choice(len(basis), size=k, replace=False)
    vec=np.zeros_like(plog)
    amp = rng.uniform(amplitude_min, amplitude_max)
    for i in idx:
        vec += rng.normal(0, amp)*basis[i]
    p = np.exp(plog + vec)
    return normalize_p(p)
```
Could also mix with uniform: `p = normalize_p(alpha*base + (1-alpha)*uniform + noise)`. But exp basis okay.

Maybe use base and best_p for restarts. Use base with large amp.

```
def fast_score(p):
    n=p.size
    c=fast_convolve_arr(p)
    cmax=float(c.max())
    if cmax <=0: return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*cmax)
```

`fast_convolve_arr(p, size=None)`:
```
def fast_convolve_arr(p, size=None):
    n=int(p.size)
    target=2*n-1
    if size is None or size < target: size=next_pow2(target)
    f=np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(f*f, size)[:target]
    # clean
    if c.size:
        cmax=float(c.max())
        if cmax > 0:
            c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
            # If FFT weird negative large? set negative to 0.
        else:
            c = np.zeros(target)
    return c
```
If cmax negative (all negative due to error?), set zeros. But p nonnegative, cmax positive.

`fast_c_var(c,n,mean=None)`:
```
def c_scores(c,n):
    cmax=float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    if cmax <=0: return 0.0, np.inf, 0.0
    mean = float(c.mean())
    var = float(np.sum((c-mean)**2))
    return cmax, float(2.0*n*cmax), var
```

```
       sc=fast_score(p); return p,sc,p,sc

                if improved: continue
```

Let's detail each.

`next_pow2(x)`:
```
def next_pow2(x):
    n = 1
    while n < int(x):
        n <<= 1
    return n
```

`normalize_p`:
```
def normalize_p(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p) & (p > 0.0), p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full_like(p, 1.0 / float(p.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return p / s
```

`fast_convolve_arr`:
```
def fast_convolve_arr(p, size=None):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    target = 2 * n - 1
    if size is None or size < target:
        size = next_pow2(target)
    f = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f, size)[:target]
    if c.size:
        cmax = float(c.max())
        if cmax <= 0.0:
            c = np.zeros(target, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c
```
If p sum not 1, c scale; okay.

`fast_score`:
```
def fast_score(p):
    n=int(p.size); c=fast_convolve_arr(p); cmax=float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    if cmax <=0: return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*cmax)
```

`analytic_base(n)`:
```
def analytic_base(n):
    a=np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n>1:
        a[0]=1.0
        val=1.0
        for k in range(1,n):
            val *= (2.0*k - 1.0)/(2.0*k)
            a[k]=val
    return normalize_p(a)
```

`make_basis(n)`:
```
def make_basis(n):
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    denom = float(max(1, n-1))
    t = idx / denom
    t = t - np.mean(t)
    basis = [t.copy()]
    basis.append((t*t - np.mean(t*t)))
    st = np.sqrt(np.maximum(0.0, idx/(denom) + 1e-12))
    basis.append(st - np.mean(st))
    freqs = [1,2,3,5,8,13]
    for m in freqs:
       phase = 2.0*np.pi*m*idx/float(n)
       c = np.cos(phase); s=np.sin(phase)
       basis.append(c - np.mean(c))
       basis.append(s - np.mean(s))
    alt = 1.0 - 2.0*( (np.arange(n) % 2).astype(np.float64) )
    basis.append(alt - np.mean(alt))
    # alt * original t (not centered t? use idx/denom)
    t_orig = idx/denom
    basis.append(alt*t_orig - np.mean(alt*t_orig))
    half = np.where(idx < n/2.0, 1.0, -1.0)
    basis.append(half - np.mean(half))
    return basis
```
`np.mean` top-level okay. For n=4096, `alt*t_orig` mean.

`make_fourier_p(params,basis,base_log)`:
```
def make_fourier_p(params, basis, base_log):
    logp = base_log.copy()
    for j in range(len(params)):
        if params[j] != 0.0:
            logp = logp + params[j] * basis[j]
    maxv = float(np.max(logp))
    logp = logp - maxv
    logp = np.where(np.isfinite(logp), logp, -50.0)
    logp = np.clip(logp, -50.0, 0.0)
    p = np.exp(logp)
    return normalize_p(p)
```
If base_log has -inf? base positive so no.

`fourier_search(n, base, basis, rng, deadline)`:
```
def fourier_search(n, base, basis, rng, deadline):
    base = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64)
    base_log = np.log(np.maximum(base, 1e-300))
    D = len(basis)
    best_params = np.zeros(D, dtype=np.float64)
    best_p = normalize_p(base)
    best_score = fast_score(best_p)
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline or D == 0:
        return best_p, best_score
    step = 0.25
    no_improve = 0
    evals = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        coord = int(rng.integers(0, D))
        # Try a few deltas
        best_local_params = best_params.copy()
        best_local_score = best_score
        best_local_p = best_p
        for sign in (-1.0, 1.0):
            for mult in (0.25, 1.0, 3.0):
                trial = best_params.copy()
                trial[coord] = best_params[coord] + sign * step * mult
                p = make_fourier_p(trial, basis, base_log)
                sc = fast_score(p)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_local_score - 1e-12:
                    best_local_score = sc; best_local_params = trial.copy(); best_local_p = p.copy()
                evals += 1
        if best_local_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_params = best_local_params; best_score=best_local_score; best_p=best_local_p; no_improve=0
        else:
            no_improve +=1
            if no_improve > 30:
                step *= 0.7
                no_improve = 0
        # random small jump occasionally
        if rng.random() < 0.05:
            jump = rng.normal(0.0, step, size=D)
            jump[int(rng.integers(0,D))] *= 3.0  # maybe
            trial=best_params+jump
            p=make_fourier_p(trial,basis,base_log); sc=fast_score(p)
            if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_params=trial; best_score=sc; best_p=p; no_improve=0
        evals +=1
        if step < 1e-4: break
    return best_p, best_score
```
This coordinate search uses many `fast_score` (6 per coord). Could be okay. But base_log from base only; if local search found better p not on this param manifold, not used. Fine.

Potential issue: `make_fourier_p` can overflow if params large, but clip. Good.

Maybe step start 0.5 better. But coordinate line search with mult up to3 gives ±step*3. If step 0.5, amplitude up to1.5. Good.

`soft_weights(c,beta)`:
```
def soft_weights(c,beta):
    c=np.asarray(c,float)
    cmax=float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    if cmax <=0 or beta <=0:
        return np.full(c.size, 1.0/float(max(1,c.size)))
    x = (c - cmax) / (cmax / beta)
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(x)
    s=float(w.sum())
    if s <=0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       w=np.zeros(c.size); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else: w/=s
    return w
```
If beta large, tau small, only exact max weight 1, tail maybe 0. Good.

`corr_fft_spec(spec_p, size, w, n)`:
```
def corr_fft_spec(spec_p, size, w, n):
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    if w.size != 2*n-1:
       # truncate/pad?
       tmp=np.zeros(2*n-1); m=min(w.size,tmp.size); tmp[:m]=w[:m]; w=tmp
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], size)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fw * spec_p, size)[:3*n-2]
    g = conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
    return 2.0 * np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
```
If size not large enough? caller passes correct. If n=1, 3n-2=1, slice [0:1]. okay.

`corr_fft(p,w)`: for external maybe:
```
def corr_fft(p,w):
    n=int(p.size); size=next_pow2(3*n-2); spec=np.fft.rfft(p,size); return corr_fft_spec(spec,size,w,n)
```

`sparse_gradient`:
```
def sparse_gradient(p, lags_weights):
    n=int(p.size); g=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for item in lags_weights:
        k = int(item[0]); wv=float(item[1])
        if wv == 0.0: continue
        lo = int(max(0, k - (n - 1)))
        hi = int(min(n - 1, k))
        if hi < lo: continue
        js = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
        idx = k - js
        g[lo:hi+1] += 2.0 * wv * p[idx]
    return g
```
`p[idx]` returns copy, okay. `idx` int64 nonnegative <n by lo/hi.

`multiplicative_update`:
```
def multiplicative_update(p, g, exp_range):
    p=np.asarray(p,float); g=np.asarray(g,float)
    if g.size != p.size: return p.copy()
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
       g=np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    gmin=float(np.min(g)); gmax=float(np.max(g)); grange=gmax-gmin
    if grange <= 1e-18:
       return p.copy()
    gs=(g-gmin)/grange
    fac=np.exp(-float(exp_range)*gs)
    pnew=p*fac
    return normalize_p(pnew)
```
If exp_range negative? use positive.

`random_basis_perturbation`:
```
def random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amplitude, max_modes=None):
    if basis is None or len(basis)==0:
        return rng.normal(0.0, amplitude, size=n)
    b=len(basis)
    if max_modes is None or max_modes > b:
        k = int(rng.integers(1, b+1))
    else:
        k = int(rng.integers(1, int(max_modes)+1))
    idx = rng.choice(b, size=k, replace=False)
    vec = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in idx:
        vec += float(rng.normal(0.0, amplitude)) * basis[i]
    return vec
```

`evaluate_candidate_choice`:
```
def evaluate_candidate_choice(cand, cur_var, best_score, n, margin):
    c = fast_convolve_arr(cand)
    if c.size == 0:
       return cand, c, 0.0, np.inf, np.inf, False, False
    cmax=float(c.max())
    if cmax <=0:
       return cand, c, 0.0, np.inf, np.inf, False, False
    max_score=float(2.0*n*cmax)
    mean=float(c.mean())
    var=float(np.sum((c-mean)**2))
    accept_max = max_score < best_score - 1e-12
    accept_var = (not accept_max) and (np.isfinite(max_score)) and (max_score <= best_score + margin) and (var < cur_var - 1e-12)
    return cand, c, mean, max_score, var, accept_max, accept_var
```
It doesn't use current c/mean. Good.

`local_search` with these helpers. Need ensure `random_basis_perturbation` called if basis list not None. If basis None, it returns Gaussian with amplitude maybe; okay.

Write local_search carefully:
```
def local_search(n, p, rng, deadline, basis):
    p = normalize_p(np.array(p, dtype=np.float64, copy=True))
    initial_score = fast_score(p)
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
       return p, initial_score, p, initial_score
    c = fast_convolve_arr(p)
    mean = float(c.mean()) if c.size else 0.0
    cmax = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    max_score = 2.0*n*cmax if cmax >0 else np.inf
    var = float(np.sum((c-mean)**2)) if c.size else 0.0
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = max_score
    best_var = var
    cur_var = var
    no_improve = 0
    beta_soft = 10.0
    corr_size = next_pow2(3*n-2)
    L = 2*n-1
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       it +=1
       spec_p = np.fft.rfft(p, corr_size)
       improved = False
       if it < 1000:
           if it %2 ==0:
               w = c - mean
               ranges = (0.03, 0.1, 0.3, 0.8)
           else:
               dev = c - mean
               w = dev * np.abs(dev)
               ranges = (0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3)
           g = corr_fft_spec(spec_p, corr_size, w, n)
           margin = 0.03 + 0.005*min(float(no_improve), 40.0)
           for er in ranges:
               cand = multiplicative_update(p, g, er)
               cand, c2, mean2, max2, var2, acc_max, acc_var = evaluate_candidate_choice(cand, cur_var, best_score, n, margin)
               if acc_max:
                   p = cand; c = c2; mean = mean2; max_score = max2; cur_var = var2
                   best_p = cand.copy(); best_score = max2; best_var = var2; no_improve=0; improved=True; break
               elif acc_var:
                   p = cand; c = c2; mean = mean2; max_score = max2; cur_var = var2; no_improve=0; improved=True; break
           if improved: continue
       else:
           rot = it % 4
           margin = 0.03 + 0.005*min(float(no_improve), 40.0)
           if rot == 0:
               w = c - mean
               g = corr_fft_spec(spec_p, corr_size, w, n)
               ranges = (0.05,0.2,0.6)
               for er in ranges: ...
           elif rot ==1:
               dev=c-mean; w=dev*np.abs(dev); g=...; ranges=(0.02,0.08,0.25)
           elif rot ==2:
               w=soft_weights(c,beta_soft); g=...; ranges=(0.1,0.3,1.0)
           else:
               K=int(rng.integers(3,13))
               idx=rng.choice(L,size=K,replace=False)
               idx[0]=int(np.argmax(c))
               wvals=np.maximum(c[idx],1e-300)
               wvals=np.power(wvals,8)
               wsz=float(wvals.sum())
               if wsz>0: wvals/=wsz
               else: wvals.fill(1.0/K)
               g=sparse_gradient(p, list(zip(idx,wvals)))
               for er in (0.03,0.1,0.3,1.0): ...
       # If still not improved, perturb
       no_improve += 1
       # structured perturbation
       amp = 0.05 + 0.02*min(float(no_improve), 30.0)
       vec = random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amp, 6)
       cand = normalize_p(p * np.exp(vec))
       cand,c2,mean2,max2,var2,acc_max,acc_var = evaluate_candidate_choice(cand, cur_var, best_score, n, margin)
       if acc_max:
           ... improved=True; no_improve=0
       elif acc_var:
           ... no_improve=0
       # if no_improve large, bigger random Gaussian
       if not improved and no_improve > 20:
           amp = 0.3 + 0.1*min(float(no_improve), 40.0)
           vec = random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amp, 10)
           cand=normalize_p(p*np.exp(vec)); evaluate...
       if not improved and no_improve > 150:
           p = best_p.copy()
           c = fast_convolve_arr(p)
           mean = float(c.mean()); max_score=...; cur_var=best_var
           no_improve = 0
       if it % 25 ==0:
           beta_soft = float(rng.uniform(1.0, 100.0))
       # optional break if best_score very low? no.
    c = fast_convolve_arr(p); cur_score=2*n*float(c.max()) if c.size else np.inf
    return best_p, best_score, p, cur_score
```

For each g,ranges, call a top-level `apply_gradient_update` that returns a tuple of results. Let's design a clean helper:

```
def apply_gradient_update(p, g, ranges, cur_var, best_p, best_score, best_var, n, margin):
    for er in ranges:
        cand = multiplicative_update(p, g, er)
        cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new, acc_max, acc_var = evaluate_candidate_choice(cand, cur_var, best_score, n, margin)
        if acc_max:
            best_p_new = cand.copy(); best_score_new = max_new; best_var_new = var_new
            return cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new, best_p_new, best_score_new, best_var_new, 0
        if acc_var:
            best_p_new = best_p
            best_score_new = best_score
            best_var_new = best_var
            return cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new, best_p_new, best_score_new, best_var_new, 0
    return p, None, 0.0, np.inf, 0.0, best_p, best_score, best_var, 1
```
Returns no_improve decrement? Actually if improved, no_improve=0; else keep. Return `improved` bool, and new no_improve maybe. We can return `new_no` =0 if improved else 1? The caller had no_improve and may increment. Let's return `improved`, and if improved set no=0 else caller increments.

```
def apply_gradient_update(p, g, ranges, cur_var, best_p, best_score, best_var, n, margin):
    for er in ranges:
        cand = multiplicative_update(p, g, er)
        cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new, acc_max, acc_var = evaluate_candidate_choice(cand, cur_var, best_score, n, margin)
        if acc_max:
            return cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new, cand.copy(), max_new, var_new, True
        if acc_var:
            return cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new, best_p, best_score, best_var, True
    return p, None, 0.0, np.inf, 0.0, best_p, best_score, best_var, False
```
If acc_var, best_p unchanged but current p becomes cand. Good.

`append_candidate`:
```
def append_candidate(p, ps, scores):
    if p is None: return
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size==0: return
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)): return
    p=normalize_p(p)
    sc=fast_score(p)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
       ps.append(p.copy()); scores.append(float(sc))
```

`scale_sequence`:
```
def scale_sequence(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64); n=int(p.size)
    if n ==0: return []
    p=np.where(np.isfinite(p)&(p>0),p,0.0)
    mx=float(p.max()); s=float(p.sum())
    if mx <=0 or s <=0:
        return [1.0]*n
    scale=float(n)
    if mx > 1000.0/scale: scale=1000.0/mx
    if s*scale < 0.01: scale=0.01/s
    if mx > 1000.0/scale: scale=1000.0/mx
    if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <=0: scale=1.0
    return [float(x*scale) for x in p]
```
If n large, list length 4096. fine.

```
               x_old=np.linspace(0.0, arr.size-1, arr.size)

   except Exception:

   t1 = start + 0.30*total

   append_candidate(p_a, ps, scores); append_candidate(p_b, ps, scores)
   t2 = t1 + 0.25*total

   append_candidate(p_four, ps, scores)
   t3 = t2 + 0.25*total

       p_start = p_a
   else:
       p_start = p_four

   append_candidate(p_c, ps, scores); append_candidate(p_d, ps, scores)
   restart_deadline = deadline - 20.0
   remaining = restart_deadline - time.monotonic()
   if remaining > 90.0:
      n_restarts = max(1, int(remaining // 60.0))
      per = remaining / float(n_restarts)
      for r in range(n_restarts):
         if time.monotonic() >= restart_deadline: break
         amp = 0.4 + 0.2*float(r % 5) + float(rng.random())*0.5
         vec = random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amp, 12)
         p0 = normalize_p(base * np.exp(vec))
         dl = min(restart_deadline, time.monotonic() + per)
         p_e, se, p_f, sf = local_search(n, p0, rng, dl, basis)
         append_candidate(p_e, ps, scores); append_candidate(p_f, ps, scores)
   # top candidates
   records=[]
   if scores:
      used=[False]*len(scores)
      for _ in range(min(12, len(scores))):
         best_i=-1; best_v=np.inf
         for i in range(len(scores)):
            if (not used[i]) and scores[i] < best_v:
               best_v=scores[i]; best_i=i
         if best_i <0: break
         used[best_i]=True; records.append(ps[best_i])
   best_seq=None; best_val=np.inf
   for p in records:
      seq=scale_sequence(p)
      if not seq: break
      val=evaluate_sequence(seq)
      if isinstance(val, float) or isinstance(val, int): pass
      if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_val:
         best_val=float(val); best_seq=seq
   if best_seq is None:
      best_seq=scale_sequence(base)
   return best_seq
```

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative sequences minimizing
  2*n*max(conv(p,p)) / sum(p)^2.

The program starts from the discrete square-root-of-flat-spectrum seed
  p_k proportional to C(2k,k)/4^k,
which already has score about pi/2, and then uses:
  * signed variance-flattening multiplicative updates,
  * soft-max peak-directed updates,
  * sparse constraint-directed updates,
  * structured low-dimensional Fourier/basis perturbations,
  * several restarts.
Final candidates are checked with the provided evaluate_sequence().
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def next_pow2(x):
    n = 1
    x = int(x)
    while n < x:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def normalize_p(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p) & (p > 0.0), p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full_like(p, 1.0 / float(p.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return p / s


def fast_convolve_arr(p, size=None):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    target = 2 * n - 1
    if size is None or size < target:
        size = next_pow2(target)
    f = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f, size)[:target]
    if c.size:
        cmax = float(c.max())
        if cmax <= 0.0:
            c = np.zeros(target, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            c = np.where(np.isfinite(c) & (c > 0.0), c, 0.0)
    return c


def fast_score(p):
    n = int(np.asarray(p).size)
    c = fast_convolve_arr(p)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax)


def analytic_base(n):
    a = np.ones(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 1:
        return normalize_p(a)
    val = 1.0
    a[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, int(n)):
        val *= (2.0 * float(k) - 1.0) / (2.0 * float(k))
        a[k] = val
    return normalize_p(a)


def make_basis(n):
    n = int(n)
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    denom = float(max(1, n - 1))
    t0 = idx / denom

    t = t0 - float(np.mean(t0))
    basis = [t.copy()]

    t2 = (t0 * t0) - float(np.mean(t0 * t0))
    basis.append(t2)

    st = np.sqrt(np.maximum(0.0, t0 + 1e-12))
    st = st - float(np.mean(st))
    basis.append(st)

    freqs = [1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 13]
    for m in freqs:
        phase = 2.0 * np.pi * float(m) * idx / float(n)
        cb = np.cos(phase)
        sb = np.sin(phase)
        basis.append(cb - float(np.mean(cb)))
        basis.append(sb - float(np.mean(sb)))

    alt = 1.0 - 2.0 * (np.arange(n) % 2).astype(np.float64)
    alt = alt - float(np.mean(alt))
    basis.append(alt)

    alt_t = (alt * t0) - float(np.mean(alt * t0))
    basis.append(alt_t)

    half = np.where(idx < 0.5 * float(n), 1.0, -1.0)
    half = half - float(np.mean(half))
    basis.append(half)

    return basis


def make_fourier_p(params, basis, base_log):
    logp = base_log.copy()
    for j in range(len(params)):
        if params[j] != 0.0:
            logp = logp + params[j] * basis[j]
    maxv = float(np.max(logp))
    logp = logp - maxv
    logp = np.where(np.isfinite(logp), logp, -50.0)
    logp = np.clip(logp, -50.0, 0.0)
    p = np.exp(logp)
    return normalize_p(p)


def fourier_search(n, base, basis, rng, deadline):
    base = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64)
    base = normalize_p(base)
    base_log = np.log(np.maximum(base, 1e-300))
    D = len(basis)

    best_params = np.zeros(D, dtype=np.float64)
    best_p = base.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(best_p)

    if D == 0 or time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return best_p, float(best_score)

    step = 0.25
    no_improve = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        coord = int(rng.integers(0, D))

        local_best_params = best_params.copy()
        local_best_p = best_p
        local_best_score = best_score

        for sign in (-1.0, 1.0):
            for mult in (0.25, 1.0, 3.0):
                trial = best_params.copy()
                trial[coord] = best_params[coord] + sign * step * mult
                p_try = make_fourier_p(trial, basis, base_log)
                sc = fast_score(p_try)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < local_best_score - 1e-12:
                    local_best_score = float(sc)
                    local_best_params = trial.copy()
                    local_best_p = p_try

        if local_best_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_params = local_best_params
            best_p = local_best_p
            best_score = local_best_score
            no_improve = 0
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 30:
                step *= 0.7
                no_improve = 0

        if rng.random() < 0.05:
            jump = rng.normal(0.0, step, size=D)
            jump[int(rng.integers(0, D))] *= 3.0
            trial = best_params + jump
            p_try = make_fourier_p(trial, basis, base_log)
            sc = fast_score(p_try)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_params = trial
                best_p = p_try
                best_score = float(sc)
                no_improve = 0

        if step < 1e-4:
            step = 0.35
            no_improve = 0

    return best_p, float(best_score)


def soft_weights(c, beta):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c) & (c >= 0.0), c, 0.0)
    m = c.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 0.0 or beta <= 0.0:
        w = np.full(m, 1.0 / float(m), dtype=np.float64)
        return w

    tau = cmax / float(beta)
    x = (c - cmax) / tau
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(x)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= s
    return w


def corr_fft_spec(spec_p, size, w, n):
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    target_len = 2 * int(n) - 1
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w) & (w != 0.0), w, 0.0)
    if w.size != target_len:
        tmp = np.zeros(target_len, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(w.size, target_len)
        if m > 0:
            tmp[:m] = w[:m]
        w = tmp

    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], size)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fw * spec_p, size)

    full_len = 3 * int(n) - 2
    conv = conv[:full_len]
    g = conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]
    return 2.0 * np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)


def corr_fft(p, w):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    size = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    spec_p = np.fft.rfft(p, size)
    return corr_fft_spec(spec_p, size, w, n)


def sparse_gradient(p, lags_weights):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    for item in lags_weights:
        k = int(item[0])
        wv = float(item[1])
        if wv == 0.0:
            continue

        lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        hi = min(n - 1, k)
        if hi < lo:
            continue

        js = np.arange(lo, hi + 1, dtype=np.int64)
        idx = np.int64(k) - js
        g[lo:hi + 1] += 2.0 * wv * p[idx]

    return g


def multiplicative_update(p, g, exp_range):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)

    if g.size != p.size:
        return p.copy()

    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g) & (g != 0.0), g, 0.0)

    gmin = float(np.min(g))
    gmax = float(np.max(g))
    grange = gmax - gmin

    if grange <= 1e-18:
        return p.copy()

    gs = (g - gmin) / grange
    fac = np.exp(-float(exp_range) * gs)
    return normalize_p(p * fac)


def random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amplitude, max_modes=None):
    n = int(n)
    if basis is None or len(basis) == 0:
        return rng.normal(0.0, amplitude, size=n)

    b = len(basis)
    if max_modes is None or int(max_modes) > b:
        k = int(rng.integers(1, b + 1))
    else:
        k = int(rng.integers(1, int(max_modes) + 1))

    idx = rng.choice(b, size=k, replace=False)
    vec = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in idx:
        vec += float(rng.normal(0.0, amplitude)) * basis[i]
    return vec


def evaluate_candidate_choice(cand, cur_var, best_score, n, margin):
    c = fast_convolve_arr(cand)
    if c.size == 0:
        return cand, c, 0.0, np.inf, np.inf, False, False

    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 0.0:
        return cand, c, 0.0, np.inf, np.inf, False, False

    max_score = float(2.0 * int(n) * cmax)
    mean = float(c.mean())
    var = float(np.sum((c - mean) ** 2))

    accept_max = max_score < best_score - 1e-12
    accept_var = (
        (not accept_max)
        and np.isfinite(max_score)
        and (max_score <= best_score + margin)
        and (var < cur_var - 1e-12)
    )

    return cand, c, mean, max_score, var, accept_max, accept_var


def apply_gradient_update(p, g, ranges, cur_var, best_p, best_score, best_var, n, margin):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    for er in ranges:
        cand = multiplicative_update(p, g, er)
        (
            cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new,
            acc_max, acc_var
        ) = evaluate_candidate_choice(cand, cur_var, best_score, n, margin)

        if acc_max:
            return (
                cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new,
                cand.copy(), max_new, var_new, True
            )
        if acc_var:
            return (
                cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new,
                best_p, best_score, best_var, True
            )

    return p, None, 0.0, np.inf, 0.0, best_p, best_score, best_var, False


def apply_direct_update(cand, cur_var, best_p, best_score, best_var, n, margin):
    (
        cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new,
        acc_max, acc_var
    ) = evaluate_candidate_choice(cand, cur_var, best_score, n, margin)

    if acc_max:
        return (
            cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new,
            cand.copy(), max_new, var_new, True
        )
    if acc_var:
        return (
            cand, c_new, mean_new, max_new, var_new,
            best_p, best_score, best_var, True
        )

    return cand, None, 0.0, np.inf, 0.0, best_p, best_score, best_var, False


def local_search(n, p, rng, deadline, basis):
    n = int(n)
    p = normalize_p(np.array(p, dtype=np.float64, copy=True))

    init_score = fast_score(p)
    if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return p, float(init_score), p, float(init_score)

    c = fast_convolve_arr(p)
    if c.size == 0:
        return p, np.inf, p, np.inf

    mean = float(c.mean())
    cmax = float(c.max())
    max_score = float(2.0 * n * cmax) if cmax > 0.0 else np.inf
    var = float(np.sum((c - mean) ** 2))

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = max_score
    best_var = var
    cur_var = var

    no_improve = 0
    beta_soft = 10.0
    it = 0

    corr_size = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    L = 2 * n - 1

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1
        spec_p = np.fft.rfft(p, corr_size)
        margin = 0.03 + 0.005 * min(float(no_improve), 40.0)
        improved = False

        if it < 1000:
            if it % 2 == 0:
                w = c - mean
                ranges = (0.05, 0.2, 0.6)
            else:
                dev = c - mean
                w = dev * np.abs(dev)
                ranges = (0.02, 0.08, 0.25)

            g = corr_fft_spec(spec_p, corr_size, w, n)
            (
                p, c, mean, max_score, cur_var,
                best_p, best_score, best_var, improved
            ) = apply_gradient_update(
                p, g, ranges, cur_var, best_p, best_score, best_var, n, margin
            )
            if improved:
                no_improve = 0
                continue
        else:
            rot = it % 4

            if rot == 0:
                w = c - mean
                ranges = (0.05, 0.2, 0.6)
                g = corr_fft_spec(spec_p, corr_size, w, n)

            elif rot == 1:
                dev = c - mean
                w = dev * np.abs(dev)
                ranges = (0.02, 0.08, 0.25)
                g = corr_fft_spec(spec_p, corr_size, w, n)

            elif rot == 2:
                w = soft_weights(c, beta_soft)
                ranges = (0.1, 0.3, 1.0)
                g = corr_fft_spec(spec_p, corr_size, w, n)

            else:
                K = int(rng.integers(3, 13))
                idx = rng.choice(L, size=K, replace=False)
                idx[0] = int(np.argmax(c))

                wvals = np.maximum(c[idx], 1e-300)
                wvals = np.power(wvals, 8)
                wsz = float(wvals.sum())
                if wsz > 0.0:
                    wvals /= wsz
                else:
                    wvals.fill(1.0 / float(K))

                g = sparse_gradient(p, list(zip(idx, wvals)))
                ranges = (0.03, 0.1, 0.3, 1.0)

            (
                p, c, mean, max_score, cur_var,
                best_p, best_score, best_var, improved
            ) = apply_gradient_update(
                p, g, ranges, cur_var, best_p, best_score, best_var, n, margin
            )

            if improved:
                no_improve = 0
                continue

        no_improve += 1
        margin = 0.03 + 0.005 * min(float(no_improve), 40.0)

        amp = 0.05 + 0.02 * min(float(no_improve), 30.0)
        vec = random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amp, 6)
        cand = normalize_p(p * np.exp(vec))

        (
            p, c, mean, max_score, cur_var,
            best_p, best_score, best_var, improved
        ) = apply_direct_update(
            cand, cur_var, best_p, best_score, best_var, n, margin
        )

        if improved:
            no_improve = 0
            continue

        if no_improve > 20:
            amp = 0.3 + 0.1 * min(float(no_improve), 40.0)
            vec = random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amp, 12)
            cand = normalize_p(p * np.exp(vec))

            (
                p, c, mean, max_score, cur_var,
                best_p, best_score, best_var, improved
            ) = apply_direct_update(
                cand, cur_var, best_p, best_score, best_var, n, margin
            )

            if improved:
                no_improve = 0
                continue

        if no_improve > 150:
            p = best_p.copy()
            c = fast_convolve_arr(p)
            mean = float(c.mean()) if c.size else 0.0
            max_score = float(best_score)
            cur_var = float(best_var)
            no_improve = 0

        if it % 25 == 0:
            beta_soft = float(rng.uniform(1.0, 100.0))

    c = fast_convolve_arr(p)
    if c.size == 0:
        cur_score = np.inf
    else:
        cmax = float(c.max())
        cur_score = float(2.0 * n * cmax) if cmax > 0.0 else np.inf

    return best_p, float(best_score), p, cur_score


def append_candidate(p, ps, scores):
    if p is None:
        return
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
        return

    p = normalize_p(p)
    if np.all(p <= 0.0):
        return

    sc = fast_score(p)
    if np.isfinite(sc):
        ps.append(p.copy())
        scores.append(float(sc))


def scale_sequence(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return []

    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p) & (p > 0.0), p, 0.0)
    mx = float(p.max())
    s = float(p.sum())

    if mx <= 0.0 or s <= 0.0:
        return [1.0] * n

    scale = float(n)

    if mx > 1000.0 / scale:
        scale = 1000.0 / mx

    if s * scale < 0.01:
        scale = 0.01 / s

    if mx > 1000.0 / scale:
        scale = 1000.0 / mx

    if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale <= 0.0:
        scale = 1.0

    return [float(x * scale) for x in p]


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Main entry point. Returns the best valid coefficient list found."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start = time.monotonic()
    total = max(10.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    deadline = start + total

    n = 4096
    base = analytic_base(n)
    basis = make_basis(n)

    ps = []
    scores = []

    append_candidate(base, ps, scores)

    # If a previous proposed sequence is available, resample it as another seed.
    try:
        hs = height_sequence_1
        if hs is not None:
            arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr) & (arr > 0.0), arr, 0.0)
            if arr.size > 0 and float(arr.sum()) > 0.0:
                if arr.size != n:
                    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, float(arr.size - 1), arr.size)
                    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, float(n - 1), n)
                    arr2 = np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)
                else:
                    arr2 = arr.copy()
                append_candidate(normalize_p(arr2), ps, scores)
    except Exception:
        arr2 = None

    # First strong local search from the analytic seed.
    t1 = start + 0.30 * total
    p_a, sa, p_b, sb = local_search(n, base, rng, t1, basis)
    append_candidate(p_a, ps, scores)
    append_candidate(p_b, ps, scores)

    # Low-dimensional Fourier/structured global-ish search.
    t2 = t1 + 0.25 * total
    p_four, s_four = fourier_search(n, base, basis, rng, t2)
    append_candidate(p_four, ps, scores)

    # Polish the better of the current best and the Fourier result.
    t3 = t2 + 0.25 * total
    if np.isfinite(sa) and (not np.isfinite(s_four) or sa <= s_four):
        p_start = p_a
    else:
        p_start = p_four

    p_c, sc_c, p_d, sd_d = local_search(n, p_start, rng, t3, basis)
    append_candidate(p_c, ps, scores)
    append_candidate(p_d, ps, scores)

    # A few restarts with larger structured perturbations.
    restart_deadline = deadline - 20.0
    remaining = restart_deadline - time.monotonic()

    if remaining > 90.0:
        n_restarts = max(1, int(remaining // 60.0))
        per = remaining / float(n_restarts)

        for r in range(n_restarts):
            if time.monotonic() >= restart_deadline:
                break

            amp = 0.4 + 0.2 * float(r % 5) + float(rng.random()) * 0.5
            vec = random_basis_perturbation(basis, rng, n, amp, 12)
            p0 = normalize_p(base * np.exp(vec))

            dl = min(restart_deadline, time.monotonic() + per)
            p_e, se, p_f, sf = local_search(n, p0, rng, dl, basis)

            append_candidate(p_e, ps, scores)
            append_candidate(p_f, ps, scores)

    # Directly verify the best internal candidates.
    records = []
    if scores:
        used = [False] * len(scores)
        select_k = min(10, len(scores))
        for _ in range(select_k):
            best_i = -1
            best_v = np.inf
            for i in range(len(scores)):
                if (not used[i]) and scores[i] < best_v:
                    best_v = scores[i]
                    best_i = i
            if best_i < 0:
                break
            used[best_i] = True
            records.append(ps[best_i])

    best_seq = None
    best_val = np.inf

    for p in records:
        seq = scale_sequence(p)
        if not seq:
            continue
        val = evaluate_sequence(seq)
        if np.isfinite(val) and val < best_val:
            best_val = float(val)
            best_seq = seq

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = scale_sequence(base)

    return best_seq
```
